Module 10: Reporting, Compliance & Finalisation
Purpose: To define all statutory reporting obligations and final processing rules.
Scope note (dev-team filtering rule). This module gives the summary table and the mechanics. It does not reproduce field-level specification content. For layouts, code tables, validation rules, XML paths and field-by-field detail, see the Colombia Report Specifications CO-RPT-001 to CO-RPT-007 and the Statutory Integrations Register CO-SIR-001 to CO-SIR-007.
10.1 Statutory Reporting
Describe the country's primary digital payroll reporting system.
Colombia's primary digital payroll reporting system is the Electronic Payroll Support Document (Documento Soporte de Pago de Nómina Electrónica), implemented by Resolution DIAN 000013 of 11 February 2021 and its 269-page Technical Annex, currently at version 1.0 with XSD version 1.0.6.
Aspect
Detail
What it is
The electronic document that constitutes the tax support for the costs and deductions arising from an employment relationship. Without it the employer cannot deduct payroll costs for income-tax purposes
Who must issue
Every taxpayer who deducts payroll costs, in respect of payments derived from an employment or statutory relationship, and payments to pensioners at the employer's charge
Frequency
Monthly, one document per employee per month, "irrespective of whether the obliged party makes payments in different or shorter periods" (art. 3). A semi-monthly payroll therefore produces one monthly XML
Content
Three totals: accrued payroll (devengados), deducted payroll (deducciones), and the difference (art. 2), itemised by concept
Deadline
Transmitted for validation within the first 10 days of the month following the payment or credit (art. 8)
Channel
Genuine machine-to-machine — SOAP 1.2, operation SendNominaSync, payload is the base64 encoding of a ZIP containing the XML, maximum 500 KB, UTF-8 only
Authentication
X.509 certificate from a certification authority accredited by the national accreditation body, XAdES-EPES signature with a claimed role of thirdparty or supplier
Response
Synchronous base64 ApplicationResponse carrying the validation result
Unique identifier
CUNE, a 96-character SHA-384 hexadecimal, computed over the document's key fields plus the software identifier and secret PIN
Corrections
Adjustment note (NominaIndividualDeAjuste) — a replacement or cancellation document referencing the original
Version literal
The Version element must carry the exact literal V1.0: Documento Soporte de Pago de Nómina Electrónica; a mismatch is a rejection (rule NIE022)
Uniqueness
A given document number may be transmitted only once per employee (global rule 90)
Employer enablement
The issuer must be enabled by the tax authority; global rule 92 rejects any document from a non-enabled issuer
This is genuinely machine-to-machine — and it is the exception, not the rule, in Colombia. The country runs three different integration postures at once:
System
Posture
Electronic payroll support document
Machine-to-machine — SOAP web service, digital signature, synchronous response
Form 350 monthly withholding return; Form 220; the information return
Portal round-trip — prepared and submitted through the tax authority's electronic filing portal
Integrated social-security contribution return
Portal round-trip through a licensed private information operator. The employer never reaches the government directly for social security

The engine cannot report a single "gross pay" figure; each concept must map to its own element. The single most important mapping rule. The XML separates salary from non-salary at element level, with paired element names carrying an explicit S or NS suffix. The engine must therefore carry a hard salary/non-salary flag on every wage type, set at configuration time, and must not attempt to derive it at reporting time.

10.2 Integrations
Is there any mandatory inbound integration?
Yes — three, and they must not be defaulted to out of scope.
#
Inbound integration
From
Format
What the engine must do with it
1
Validation response to the electronic payroll document (ApplicationResponse)
DIAN, synchronously in the same SOAP call
base64 XML
Parse it. A document is legally issued only once validated. The engine must store the validation status and the CUNE, retry or correct rejections, and block the tax deduction of an unvalidated payroll cost. This is a hard inbound dependency, not a receipt
2
Contribution-return settlement response
The licensed information operator
Operator-specific file or portal output
Reconcile the settled contributions against the engine's computation, capture the return number and payment date, and detect rejected contributor records
3
Health-insurer and occupational-risk benefit determinations
Health insurer (EPS) and occupational-risk insurer (ARL)
Portal output or file
Determine the amount and period of the sickness, maternity, paternity and occupational-injury benefit actually recognised, so the employer's payroll advance can be reconciled and any shortfall recovered from the employee or absorbed
Two further inbound flows that are operational rather than statutory but that a production implementation will need: the severance-savings fund's confirmation of the February deposit, and the payroll lender's instalment schedule under a libranza authorisation.
Why this matters for the scope manifest. Inbound files that the engine must parse and act on carry product_mapping_required: true. Item 1 in particular is not optional — an employer that transmits without consuming the validation response cannot demonstrate that its payroll costs are supported, which is the entire purpose of the document.
Is there any mandatory outbound integration beyond standard reporting?
Yes — two, plus the payment file.
#
Outbound integration
To
Format
Nature
1
Electronic payroll document transmission
DIAN
SOAP 1.2 SendNominaSync; base64 of a ZIP containing the XML; ≤ 500 KB; UTF-8; XAdES-EPES signature with an X.509 certificate from an accredited certification authority
Genuine machine-to-machine. This goes beyond "standard reporting" — it is a real-time signed web-service call with a synchronous validation response
2
Graphical representation of the payroll document to the employee
The employee
PDF or printed
Permissive under Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 24 — but if issued, it must carry the art. 5 content and a QR code of at least 2 cm on every page
3
Bank payment file
The employer's paying bank
Bank-specific fixed-width or delimited format
Colombia does not use IBAN. Domestic account numbers are numeric, of bank-specific length (commonly 10–17 digits), with no national check-digit standard. The bank-entity code catalogue is bank- and clearing-house-specific and must be obtained per client
Third-party operation. Where Mercans generates or transmits the electronic payroll document on the client's behalf, Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 22 requires that the provider be enabled by the tax authority, be registered in the tax registry, and that both parties retain the mandate document accrediting the arrangement. This is a contractual precondition to go-live, not a technical one.
Ownership note (control B7). The following are explicitly owned upstream and are not Mercans deliverables: the licensed information operator's own file format and portal (owned by the operator); the severance-savings fund's deposit interface (owned by the fund); the payroll lender's schedule (owned by the lender); and the bank's payment-file specification (owned by the client's bank). Each implies an inbound data contract the engine must receive — respectively the settlement response, the deposit confirmation, the instalment schedule and the payment acknowledgement. Reversing any of these scope calls is a contained change to one interface, not a redesign.
10.3 Payslip Requirements
List all mandatory components that must be itemized on an employee payslip.
The headline finding: Colombia has no statutory payslip-content article in its labour code. The Substantive Labour Code was grep-tested across its full consolidated text for the seven Spanish terms used for a payslip — desprendible, comprobante de pago, colilla, volante de pago, recibo de pago, constancia de pago and detalle de la liquidación — with zero hits. Law 2466 of 2025 added none.
The operative itemisation rule is therefore tax law, not labour law: Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 article 5, which lists the mandatory content of the electronic payroll support document, combined with article 24, which makes the graphical representation permissive but requires it to carry that content if it is issued.
Mandatory content — Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 5 (13 items):
#
Item
1
Employer's or issuer's surnames and names, or corporate name, and tax identification number
2
Employee's or beneficiary's surnames and names, and identity document number
3
Identification of the electronic payroll support document — a unique consecutive number preceded by the authorised prefix
4
Generation date
5
Payment or credit date
6
Accrued payroll values (valores devengados de nómina), itemised by concept
7
Deducted payroll values (valores deducidos de nómina), itemised by concept
8
Total difference between accrued and deducted
9
Payment or credit period
10
Payment method and medium
11
Country, department and municipality of origin
12
Content of the authorised technical annex

CUNE — the unique electronic payroll code
Additional mandatory formal requirements where a graphical representation is issued:
Requirement
Rule
Language
Spanish, mandatory. Another language may be added, never substituted (art. 26)
Currency
Colombian pesos, mandatory. Another currency may be added (art. 26)
QR code
At least 2 cm × 2 cm on every page, encoding the tax authority's document-search URL with the CUNE as the key (Technical Annex §13.1)
Legal status
The paper or PDF document is a representation of the electronic document, not the document itself. Its legal validity derives from the validated XML
Mercans practice — items that are good practice but not legally required:
the itemisation of employer contributions for transparency (never a deduction from the employee);
the statutory benefit accruals and balances;
the vacation balance;
the hourly divisor and rest-day premium percentage applied, given the two mid-2026 cut-overs;
the contract type, hire date and pay frequency;
year-to-date totals.
A formatting rule that must not be blended. The payslip carries the XML figures — two decimals, round-half-to-even. It must not display the contribution-return figures (rounded up to the nearest 100) as if they were the deduction, nor the Form 350 figure (rounded to the nearest 1,000). Where the client requires the contribution-return figure to be shown, it must be shown as a separate, labelled line.
Legal basis: Resolution DIAN 000013 of 11 February 2021 arts. 5, 24, 26 and Technical Annex §13.1; Substantive Labour Code (Decree 2663 of 1950), full consolidated text — no payslip-content article.
10.4 Other Compliance
Is payroll software certification mandatory?
Not certification in the formal sense — but *enablement* is mandatory, and it functions as a gate.
Requirement
Status
Formal software certification or accreditation scheme
No. There is no Colombian payroll-software certification body, no certificate of conformity and no periodic recertification
Enablement (*habilitación*) of the issuer with the tax authority
Mandatory. Global validation rule 92 rejects any document from a non-enabled issuer. The employer, or the third-party provider acting for it, must complete the enablement process in the tax authority's test environment before transmitting in production
Software identifier and secret PIN
Mandatory. Assigned by the tax authority at software activation. The PIN never appears in the XML; it feeds both the software security code and the CUNE
Software security code
Mandatory. SHA-384 of SoftwareID + PIN + document number
Digital certificate
Mandatory. X.509 from a certification authority accredited by the national accreditation body, used for the XAdES-EPES signature
Third-party provider registration
Mandatory where a provider generates or transmits. The provider must be enabled and registered in the tax registry, and both parties must retain the mandate document (art. 22)
Contribution-return software
No certification. Filing is through a licensed information operator, and the operator's technical guide governs the file it accepts
Practical consequence. Enablement is a project prerequisite with a lead time, not a configuration step. It requires the employer's digital certificate, the tax-registry registration, and a successful pass through the test environment. It must be scheduled at the start of an implementation, not at go-live.
Other compliance obligations that are not software-related:
Obligation
Source
Overtime register — activity, total supplementary hours, day/night split, evidence of payment; available to employees and to administrative and judicial authorities
Labour Code art. 162 num. 2 (Law 2466 of 2025 art. 12); Circular 101 of 2025 §3. Failure allows inspectors to suspend all supplementary work for six months
Vacation register — entry date, start and end of each vacation, remuneration paid
Labour Code art. 187 num. 3
Internal work rules (reglamento de trabajo), published
Labour Code art. 120, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 8
Disciplinary due process before any sanction
Labour Code art. 115, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 7
Disability-hiring quota — employers with up to 500 employees must have at least two employees with a disability per 100; from 501 employees, one additional per 100-employee tranche, on the permanent headcount, with health-ministry certification. Contracts must be reported to the Ministry of Labour within 15 days of signature. The values are optional in the law's first year and mandatory from the second
Law 2466 of 2025 art. 15
Apprentice quota, or its monetisation
Law 789 of 2002 art. 34, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 23
Data-protection compliance — internal data-treatment policy, privacy notice, database registration
Law 1581 of 2012; Decree 1074 of 2015
10.5 Year-End Processing and Finalisation
Describe the mandatory year-end finalisation process.
Colombia's year end is a settlement and certification process, not a reconciliation. There is no annual tax true-up and no annual social-security reconciliation.
DECEMBER 2026  1. Pay the SECOND HALF of the service bonus         by 20 December     = (salary + transport allowance) x days in the semester / 360  2. Deliver the third WORK-CLOTHING issue            by 20 December  3. RECALCULATE the fixed withholding percentage for every employee on     Procedure 2:  (sum of 12 months' taxable payments) / 13 -> table -> %     Binds January to June 2027.  4. LIQUIDATE SEVERANCE SAVINGS at 31 December     = (salary + transport allowance) x days of service in the year / 360     Base rule (art. 253): the LAST monthly salary if unchanged in the last     3 months; otherwise the average of the last year.  5. Compute the INTEREST on the 31 December balance     = balance x 12% x days / 360  6. Run the December payroll and transmit the December XML by 10 January.JANUARY 2027  7. PAY THE INTEREST ON SEVERANCE SAVINGS TO THE EMPLOYEE, during January.     Not to the fund - directly to the employee.     Failure = an indemnity equal to the interest owed, once only.  8. REPARAMETERISE: new minimum wage, new transport allowance, new tax unit.     Every floor, ceiling, trigger and band boundary moves.  9. File the December contribution return and Form 350 on the ordinary     business-day calendar.FEBRUARY 2027 10. DEPOSIT SEVERANCE SAVINGS to each employee's chosen fund     BEFORE 15 FEBRUARY (i.e. by 14 February).     Late deposit = ONE DAY'S SALARY FOR EACH DAY OF DELAY.     Exception: where the employer proves direct payment to the employee,     that payment is discharging and no penalty applies     (art. 57 par. 4, Law 2466 of 2025 art. 15) - but fund affiliation     remains compulsory.MARCH 2027 11. ISSUE FORM 220, the annual income and withholding certificate,     to EVERY employee, by the LAST BUSINESS DAY OF MARCH.MAY-JUNE 2027 12. File the INFORMATION RETURN, Format 2276 version 4, by the deadline     applicable to the employer's tax number.
The most expensive year-end failure is the severance-savings deposit. One day's salary per day of delay accrues without cap until the deposit is made, and it is a per-employee penalty. For a workforce of 100 employees on the minimum wage, a two-week delay is roughly COP 81.7 million.
What documentation or annual statements must be provided to employees?

Is there a formal process for correcting errors discovered after the fiscal year has been closed?
Yes — four separate processes, one per authority. There is no single "prior-year adjustment" mechanism.
Error
Correction route
Time limit
Withholding-tax return (Form 350) understated
File a correction return increasing the declared value. Tax Code art. 588: within 2 years of the filing deadline; a correction penalty applies
2 years
Withholding-tax return overstated
Correction under Tax Code art. 589, which reduces the declared value. Must be filed within 1 year of the original filing deadline
1 year
Withholding practised in excess of, or otherwise than in accordance with, the law
Decree 1625 of 2016 art. 1.2.4.16: on the employee's written request with evidence, the employer reimburses the excess and deducts the reimbursed amount from the withholdings of the following period, carrying any surplus forward until absorbed. The employer retains the employee's written request
Ongoing
Social-security contributions under-declared
Correction planilla (type N): paired records, an A group carrying the originally settled values and a C group carrying the corrected values. The sum of the contribution bases and of the day counts in the C records must be greater than or equal to those in the A records. Filed only through the same information operator that carried the original return, absent an authorisation from the financial superintendence. Late-payment interest accrues from each original due date
No statutory limit; the contributions authority's assessment powers run to 5 years
Social-security contributions over-declared
The correction planilla is structurally designed for under-declaration. A genuine overpayment requires a refund claim to each receiving entity — the health insurer, pension administrator, occupational-risk insurer, family compensation fund, apprenticeship service or family welfare institute — each with its own procedure
Per entity
Electronic payroll document wrong
Adjustment note (NominaIndividualDeAjuste) — a replacement or cancellation document referencing the original by its unique identifier
Per the annex
Information return (Format 2276) wrong
Correction return through the tax authority's information-return service. Penalties apply for late, incorrect or incomplete information under Tax Code art. 651

Statutory benefits under-paid
Pay the shortfall directly to the employee, with the applicable indemnity. The limitation period for the employee's claim is 3 years from the date the obligation became enforceable (Labour Code art. 488, as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 62)
3 years
Severance savings deposited late or short
Pay the shortfall plus one day's salary for each day of delay
—
Sequencing rule. Where a single error affects more than one authority — for example an omitted retrospective salary increase — correct in this order: (1) recompute the statutory benefits and pay the employee; (2) file the social-security correction planillas for each affected month with interest; (3) declare the tax effect in the current month's Form 350, since withholding tax follows the payment date; (4) issue an adjustment note for the electronic payroll document; (5) reissue Form 220 if the year has been certified.
Legal basis: Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 378, 379, 380, 386, 588, 589, 651, 850; Decree 1625 of 2016 arts. 1.2.4.16 and 1.6.1.13.2.40; Resolution DIAN 000022 of 20 February 2024; Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 arts. 5, 22, 24, 26; Resolution DIAN 000227 of 2025; Substantive Labour Code arts. 57 (nums. 7 and par. 4), 65 par. 1, 115, 120, 162 num. 2, 187 num. 3, 232, 249, 253, 306, 488 (as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 arts. 7, 8, 12, 15, 62); Law 50 of 1990 art. 99; Law 52 of 1975 art. 1; Law 789 of 2002 art. 34 (as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 23); Resolution MSPS 2388 of 2016 Technical Annex 2; Law 1581 of 2012; Decree 1074 of 2015; Ministry of Labour Circular 101 of 2025 §3.

Module 11: Advanced Scenarios & Cross-Cutting Logic
Purpose: To capture complex, overarching rules that affect multiple aspects of payroll.
11.1 Calculation Logic
How is the proration and projection logic applied for income tax, social security, exemptions, and deductions?
Colombia prorates on a 30-day commercial month and a 360-day year, and — because the system is neither cumulative nor annualised — it projects nothing.
Element
Proration
Projection
Basic salary
S × days worked ÷ 30
None
Transport allowance
249,095 × days worked ÷ 30
None
Contribution base
On the prorated salary; the 1-minimum-wage floor is also prorated to the contributory days
None
Contribution-base ceiling
25 minimum wages, applied to the monthly base — not prorated
None
Solidarity-fund trigger
Tested on the monthly base as computed — not prorated, not annualised
None
Exemption test (art. 114-1)
Tested on the month's actual earnings — not prorated to a full-month equivalent, not averaged
None
Payroll-levy base
On the prorated salary plus paid rest days; no ceiling
None
Withholding tax, Procedure 1
Art. 385 sub-30-day rule: (payments ÷ days) × 30 → read the table percentage → apply to the actual payments
None — this is a grossing-up for rate determination, not a projection of future income
Withholding tax, Procedure 2
The fixed percentage applies to whatever the month's base is
Backward-looking only — 12 months' history ÷ 13
Annual exemption caps (790 and 1,340 tax units)
Apportioned at one twelfth per month — 65.8333 units = COP 3,447,955 and 111.6667 units = COP 5,848,430
None
Per-dependant deduction (72 tax units per year, max 4)
Apportioned at 6 tax units per dependant per month = COP 314,244
None
Monthly-capped deductions (housing interest 100 units, prepaid health 16 units, dependants 32 units)
Already monthly — no proration
None
Statutory benefit accruals
base × days ÷ 360 for the service bonus, severance savings and interest; base × days ÷ 720 for vacation
None
Vacation entitlement
1.25 working days per month of service
None
The one genuine "projection-like" mechanic is Procedure 2, and it looks backwards. The fixed percentage is derived from the preceding 12 months' taxable payments divided by 13 — never from an estimate of the year ahead. There is no projected annual salary, no notional bonus and no year-end catch-up anywhere in Colombian payroll.
A proration asymmetry worth stating. The contribution-base floor is prorated to the contributory days; the contribution-base ceiling is not. An employee hired on the 20th of a month contributes on 11 days at the prorated floor, but if that employee earns COP 60,000,000 for those 11 days the base is capped at the full monthly 25-minimum-wage ceiling of COP 43,772,625, not at 11/30 of it.
If tax or contribution rates change mid-pay period, which rates apply?
Colombia's default rule is that a statutory change applies from its stated effective date, prospectively, and a pay period spanning the date must be split. Colombian statutes are unusually explicit about effective dates, and 2026 has three.
Change
Effective date
Rule
Minimum wage
1 January
Applies from 1 January. Every derived floor, ceiling and trigger moves at once. There is no transitional or blended rate
Tax unit
1 January
Applies to the whole tax year. Every band boundary and cap reprices
Rest-day and holiday premium: 80% → 90%
1 July 2026
Split the month. Hours worked on rest days up to 30 June carry 80%; from 1 July, 90%. Circular 101 of 2025 §4
Hourly divisor: 220 → 210
15 July 2026
Split the month. The ordinary hour value to 14 July is salary ÷ 220; from 15 July it is salary ÷ 210 — a rise of about 4.8% in every premium. Circular 101 of 2025
Night window: 21:00 → 19:00
25 December 2025
Already fully effective for all of 2026
Maximum ordinary week: 44 → 42 hours
15 July 2026
Split at 15 July. Maximum ordinary plus overtime hours falls from 56 to 54 per week
Contribution rates
No change for 2026
—
Occupational-risk class or rate
On reclassification
Applies from the effective date of the reclassification; report a work-centre change event
Employee-requested higher withholding rate
The month after the request (Tax Code art. 383 par. 3)
Never mid-month
Procedure 2 fixed percentage
1 July and 1 January
Never mid-semester
July 2026 is the critical month, and it has two cut-overs two weeks apart. They must not be collapsed:
1 - 30 June 2026     rest-day premium 80%,  divisor 2201 - 14 July 2026     rest-day premium 90%,  divisor 22015 - 31 July 2026    rest-day premium 90%,  divisor 210
A rest-day hour worked on 10 July 2026 by an employee on COP 2,400,000 is therefore 2,400,000 ÷ 220 × 0.90 = COP 9,818.18 of premium, while the same hour on 20 July is 2,400,000 ÷ 210 × 0.90 = COP 10,285.71. Applying one rate to the whole month understates or overstates every premium hour in it.
The tax-authority position on reporting the split. DIAN Concepto 013840 (int. 1568) of 1 October 2025 confirms that "the possible coexistence of differentiated premium percentages within the same month does not constitute any obstacle to the generation, transmission and subsequent validation" of the electronic payroll document, and that the annex's published percentage "must be read in accordance with the rules in force for each specific case". The employer consolidates all the month's earnings, each premium settled at the percentage applicable to its own date.
How are tax and social security handled when an employee is paid multiple times in a single pay period?
Both are monthly by construction, so multiple payments are aggregated into one monthly calculation.
Aspect
Rule
Social security
One contribution base per employee per month, built from all payments attributed to the month. One set of contributions. One record on the contribution return. Floors, ceilings and triggers all tested on the aggregate
Withholding tax, Procedure 1
The table is read on the month's depurated total. Where the employer withholds on each payment, the second withholding must be computed as table(month total) − already withheld, not as a fresh read on the second payment alone. Reading the table twice on two half-month bases systematically under-withholds, because the progressive bands are applied twice from zero
Withholding tax, Procedure 2
The fixed percentage is applied to each payment, or to the month's total; both give the same result, because the percentage is linear
Electronic payroll XML
One document per employee per month, aggregating all payments — Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 3 and DIAN Concepto 013840 of 2025
Form 350
One monthly aggregate
The statutory service bonus under Procedure 1
Keeps its separate table read even if paid in the same month as salary
Severance savings and interest
Excluded from the monthly base under both procedures, whenever paid
The dominant Colombian case is the semi-monthly payroll. Market practice pays the 15th as a fixed advance of half the basic salary with no withholding, and settles the whole month on the second payment: all variable pay, the whole month's contributions and the whole month's withholding. This is arithmetically correct and is what the engine should default to. The statutorily rigorous alternative — grossing each half-month to 30 days under art. 385, reading the table percentage and applying it to the actual half-month payment — produces a smoother net and is available where a client requires it.
Off-cycle payments in the same month (a bonus paid on the 20th, a termination settlement paid on the 25th) are aggregated into the same monthly base for both tax and social security. The tax effect of an additional payment is therefore always table(new month total) − already withheld.
How is payroll calculated if an employee's pay cycle changes mid-year?
A change of pay frequency is an administrative change, not a statutory event. Colombian law neither restricts nor regulates it, beyond the outer limits of Labour Code art. 134 — one week for day wages, one month for salaries — and the general requirement that salary be paid in equal periods and in arrears.
Aspect
Effect of a frequency change
Contribution rates, floors, ceilings, triggers
None — all are monthly parameters
Contribution return
None — monthly regardless
Electronic payroll XML
None — monthly regardless. Only the pay-frequency code changes
Withholding table
None — the table is monthly
Procedure 1
The art. 385 sub-30-day rule applies to any transitional period shorter than a month
Procedure 2
None — the fixed percentage is unaffected. The 12-month accumulator simply continues
Statutory benefit accruals
None — they accrue on time, not on payments
Vacation accrual
None
Procedure for a mid-year change.
1. Change at a MONTH BOUNDARY. Both statutory artefacts are monthly and   keyed to the payment month; a mid-month change splits an indivisible filing.2. Ensure no PERIOD IS SKIPPED OR DOUBLE-PAID at the transition.   Monthly -> semi-monthly:  pay the full month on the last day, then start                             the 15th/last-day pattern in the following month.   Semi-monthly -> monthly:  pay the second half as normal, then move to a                             single month-end payment.   Weekly -> semi-monthly:   pay the outstanding weeks to the changeover date,                             then start the new pattern. A transitional period                             shorter than 30 days uses the art. 385 rule.3. Update the PAY-FREQUENCY CODE on the employee record for the XML.4. Preserve every ACCUMULATOR: 12-month taxable payments, 6-month average   labour income, year-to-date cap consumption, benefit accruals, vacation   balance. None of them resets.5. Where the contract specifies the pay date, obtain the employee's WRITTEN   AGREEMENT to the change. A unilateral change to a contractual pay date is   a variation of an essential term and, if adverse, is capable of amounting   to indirect dismissal under Labour Code art. 62 lit. B).6. Do NOT extend the interval beyond the art. 134 maxima: one week for   day wages, one month for salaries.
The one real risk is a gap or an overlap at the transition, which produces either an unpaid period — exposing the employer to the Labour Code art. 65 late-payment penalty — or a double payment that must be recovered under art. 151, requiring a written agreement with the instalment and amortisation period stated.
Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 62, 65, 127, 134, 151, 179 (as substituted by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 14), 161 (as substituted by art. 11); Law 2101 of 2021 arts. 3 and 4; Ministry of Labour Circular 101 of 22 September 2025 §§2.1, 4, 5.1; Tax Code arts. 383 par. 3, 385, 386, 388, 114-1; Law 100 of 1993 art. 18; Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 3; DIAN Concepto 013840 int. 1568 of 1 October 2025; Resolution DIAN 000238 of 2025; Decree 1469 of 2025.
11.2 Special Employee Groups & Income
How does taxation and social security differ for part-time employees and Board of Directors?
Part-time employees.
Colombia has no distinct part-time employment regime, no separate part-time contract type and no reduced part-time entitlements. A part-time employee is an ordinary employee whose agreed hours are fewer than the maximum.
Aspect
Part-time treatment
Minimum wage
Applies proportionally to time worked. There is no separate part-time rate
Contribution rates
Identical — health 4%/8.5%, pension 4%/12%, occupational risk, levies 4%/2%/3%
Contribution base
Actual earnings, but floored at 1 minimum wage where the employee works a full month on part-time hours. This is the defining part-time cost effect: an employee earning COP 900,000 for a full month of part-time work still contributes on COP 1,750,905
Statutory benefits
Full proportional entitlement — service bonus, severance savings, interest, vacation, work clothing
Paid weekly rest
Labour Code art. 173 num. 5: where the agreed schedule does not require work on all working days of the week, the rest day is paid in proportion to time worked
Withholding tax
Ordinary. In practice a part-time employee is almost always in the 0% band
Overtime
Hours beyond the agreed ordinary day are supplementary work, even where they are below the statutory maximum (Labour Code art. 159)
Part-time contributions for micro-enterprises and households
Law 2466 of 2025 art. 34 permits micro-enterprises and households to pay social security on a part-time basis, computed in weeks and settled monthly, and to contribute proportionally where monthly income is below the minimum wage. Requires commercial registration, accounting books and an internal contract register. Monitored by the contributions authority; may not be used where the work is in fact full-time (par. 2). Outside standard corporate payroll scope
Board of Directors. The treatment turns on the substance of the relationship, not the title.
Situation
Employment?
Social security
Withholding tax
Executive director holding an employment contract
Yes
Full employee treatment on the whole remuneration — salary and any director's fees that are consideration for the service. Employer 12% + 8.5% + occupational risk + levies; employee 4% + 4% + solidarity fund
Art. 383 progressive table
Non-executive director receiving attendance fees only
No — a corporate mandate, not employment
Not an employee contribution. The director contributes as an independent worker on a base of 40% of the monthly fee, floored at 1 minimum wage (Law 1955 of 2019 art. 244)
Art. 383 table still applies — art. 383 par. 2, added by Law 2277 of 2022 art. 8, extends the employment table to non-employment work income using "pagos mensualizados": total contract value less mandatory health and pension contributions, divided by the months of the contract
Statutory legal representative on an employment contract
Yes
Full employee treatment. Generally excluded from the maximum-hours rules as management staff (Labour Code art. 162 lit. a)
Art. 383 table
Two engine consequences. A person who is both an employee and a fee-earning director has two different social-security bases — full salary for the employment, 40% of the fees for the mandate — but one withholding table, and the two accumulate against the shared 25-minimum-wage ceiling. A fee-only director is outside Mercans payroll scope and is handled as an independent-contractor payment; the client retains that responsibility.
How is taxation handled for employees with multiple employers?
Social security is the constrained side; tax is not.
Aspect
Rule
Social security — the shared ceiling
Law 100 of 1993 art. 18 par. 1: where the employee has two or more employers, contributions are made proportionally to each remuneration and accumulate up to the legal ceiling of 25 minimum wages. Critically: "contributions to the health system must be made on the same base" — the health base must match the pension base
How the proportion works
Each employer contributes on its own remuneration. Where the combined base would exceed 25 minimum wages, the ceiling is allocated pro rata to each employer's share of the total remuneration. Each employer therefore needs the other employer's base, which only the employee can supply
The employee's duty
To declare the second employment and the base with the other employer. Without that declaration each employer applies the ceiling to its own base alone, and the employee over-contributes
Occupational risk
Contributed by each employer on its own base; there is no shared ceiling logic in practice, because each employer insures its own exposure at its own work centre
Payroll levies
Each employer on its own payroll-levy base; no ceiling at all, so no allocation question arises
Health and pension institutions
The employee has one health insurer and one pension administrator. Both employers pay to the same institutions
Withholding tax
Each employer withholds independently, on its own payments, as if it were the only employer. There is no aggregation, no cumulative code, no coordination and no employer duty to enquire
The consequence
Each employer applies the 0% band, the 25% exemption and the art. 387 deductions to its own payments. The employee is therefore systematically under-withheld across the two employments, and reconciles — usually with a balance to pay — on the annual income-tax return
The employee's remedy
Tax Code art. 383 par. 3: request a higher withholding rate in writing from one or both employers, effective from the month after the request. This is the intended mechanism and the engine must support it
Exemption test (art. 114-1)
Applied by each employer separately on the remuneration it pays. An employee earning 6 minimum wages from each of two employers is below 10 minimum wages at each, so both employers are exonerated, even though the employee earns 12 minimum wages in total
Configuration. Carry a multi-employer flag and, where declared, the other employer's contribution base, so that the ceiling allocation can be computed. Do not attempt to aggregate for tax.
What is the treatment of foreign-sourced employment income for residents?
A Colombian tax resident is taxed on worldwide income (Tax Code art. 9), so foreign-sourced employment income is taxable in Colombia. What matters for payroll is who pays it.
Situation
Payroll treatment
Resident employee, paid by the Colombian employer for work performed abroad
Ordinary Colombian payroll. Fully taxable under the art. 383 table, fully contributory. The place of performance does not change the treatment of a payment made by a Colombian employer to a resident
Resident employee, paid directly by a foreign group company (a "shadow payroll" or split-payroll arrangement)
The Colombian employer is a withholding agent only in respect of payments it makes. A payment made by a foreign entity with no Colombian withholding-agent status is not subject to Colombian withholding. The income remains taxable to the employee, who declares it in the annual return. Colombia has no shadow-payroll obligation requiring the local entity to withhold on foreign-paid amounts, unless the local entity in substance bears the cost, in which case the payment is treated as made by it
Resident employee with pre-arrival foreign employment income in the year of arrival
Residence attaches to the whole tax year in which the 183rd day falls, so pre-arrival income of that year is within the worldwide base. Not a payroll matter; the employee declares it
Foreign social-security contributions paid by a resident
Not deductible against the Colombian withholding base. Only Colombian mandatory pension, solidarity-fund and health contributions are non-taxable income under arts. 55 and 56
Double-tax relief
Tax Code art. 254 grants a credit for tax paid abroad on foreign-sourced income, limited to the Colombian tax on the same income. Claimed in the employee's annual return, not through payroll
Treaty relief
Colombia's treaty network and Andean Community Decision 578 may assign the taxing right to the other state. Applied only on written client instruction supported by a residence certificate
The rule of thumb for the engine: withhold on what the Colombian entity pays; report what it pays; do not attempt to gross up or shadow-withhold on foreign-paid amounts unless the client instructs it and the local entity bears the cost.
How do employer stock option plans work and how are they taxed?
Colombia has no dedicated statutory regime for employee share schemes — no approved-plan legislation, no tax-advantaged option scheme and no specific valuation rule. The treatment is derived from first principles.
Aspect
Treatment
Legal nature
A benefit derived from the employment relationship. Where it is ordinary and permanent consideration for the service it is salary in kind (Labour Code art. 129); where it is occasional or gratuitous, or subject to a valid written non-salary pact, it is not salary (art. 128)
Taxable event
Exercise (for options) or vesting (for restricted shares) — the point at which the employee acquires an unconditional economic benefit
Taxable amount
The spread: market value of the shares at exercise or vesting, less any exercise price paid by the employee
Tax character
Employment income, taxable within the ordinary monthly base under the art. 383 progressive table. There is no preferential rate
Withholding
The employer withholds. Where the benefit is non-cash and cash pay is insufficient, the minimum-wage protection rules of Module 4.1 apply and the shortfall is carried
Social security
Contributory only if the award is salary. Where a valid written non-salary pact exists, it is out of the contribution base up to the 40% clamp; the excess above 40% of total remuneration goes back in
Statutory benefits
Enter the service-bonus, severance-savings and vacation bases only if salary
Subsequent disposal
A separate transaction for the employee — occasional gain or capital income depending on the holding period. Outside payroll
Awards from a foreign parent
The Colombian entity withholds where it makes or bears the cost of the payment. Where the foreign parent grants and settles directly at its own cost, there is no Colombian withholding agent for that payment, but the income remains taxable to a resident employee, who declares it
Non-resident employee
Flat 20% under art. 408 where the underlying service was performed in Colombia; foreign-source and not taxable where it was performed abroad
Valuation
No statutory scale. Market value at the taxable event, evidenced by the plan administrator's valuation or the quoted price
Configuration. Model an equity award as a taxable earnings line carrying the spread, with an explicit salary/non-salary flag, plus an offsetting deduction line where no cash is delivered. The salary/non-salary flag decides the contribution and benefit consequences; it has no tax effect.
How is payroll handled for employees who transfer between different legal entities mid-year?
The controlling concept is substitution of employer (sustitución patronal), Labour Code arts. 67–72, which distinguishes sharply from a resignation-and-rehire.
Case A — substitution of employer (business transfer, merger, spin-off).
Aspect
Rule
Definition — art. 67
A change of employer "by virtue of an act between living persons (inter vivos)", with continuity of the business and of the employee in their post
Effect on the contract — art. 68
The employment contract is not terminated, suspended or modified. Seniority, entitlements and accruals continue unbroken
Liability — arts. 69–70
The outgoing and incoming employers are jointly and severally liable for obligations accrued before the substitution; the incoming employer alone is liable for those accruing after. They may agree between themselves on the internal allocation, without prejudice to the employee
Statutory benefits
Not settled at the transfer. Seniority runs continuously; the incoming employer inherits the accrued liability. The parties may agree to settle severance savings at the transfer date, with the employee's agreement
Social security
The incoming employer reports a hire change event and the outgoing employer a termination change event in the transfer month, under their different tax numbers. Contributions are split by days
Withholding tax
Each entity withholds independently on its own payments. There is no carry-over of year-to-date figures. Under Procedure 2 the incoming entity has no 12-month history of its own, so art. 386 directs it to read the table on the month's own payments until its first semi-annual calculation. Each entity issues its own Form 220 for the part-year it paid
Annual caps
Because each entity withholds independently, each applies the annual caps from zero. The employee may therefore over-consume the 790-unit and 1,340-unit caps across the two entities and reconcile in the annual return
Electronic payroll XML
Each entity issues its own document under its own tax number and enablement, for the part-month it paid
Occupational risk
Re-affiliation to the incoming employer's insurer; the risk class follows the incoming employer's work centre
Health insurer, pension administrator, severance-savings fund
Unchanged — these are employee elections and survive the transfer
Case B — resignation and rehire within a group (no business continuity). This is a genuine termination and a genuine new hire:
Full termination settlement by the outgoing entity — salary, severance savings, interest, service bonus,
unused vacation. No dismissal indemnity where the employee resigns.
Social-security payment evidence for the last three months, within 60 days, failing which the
termination does not take effect (art. 65 par. 1).
Seniority restarts at the incoming entity, with all the consequences for the indemnity tier, the
work-clothing qualification and the vacation accumulation limits.
Where the arrangement is in substance a device to break seniority, it is exposed to challenge —
Labour Code art. 59A, added by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 48, prohibits employer manoeuvres of evasion (maniobras de elusión).
The engine must carry a "continuity of service" flag distinguishing the two cases, because it drives seniority, the indemnity tier and whether benefits are settled at the transfer.
What is the process for an employee who changes citizenship or residency status during the fiscal year?
Tax residence change — the operationally significant case.
Direction
Trigger
Payroll effect
Non-resident → resident
The employee reaches 183 days of presence in any 365-day period
Switch from the flat 20% of art. 408 to the art. 383 progressive table, from the payment following the date the test is met. Confirmed by DIAN Concepto 011108 of 29 June 2026, which rejected withholding under art. 383 from day one in anticipation of residence: status is determined "at the time of each payment". No retroactive adjustment through payroll; the employee reconciles in the annual return
Resident → non-resident
Departure such that the 183-day test is no longer met for the relevant period
Switch to the flat 20% for Colombian-source payments; no withholding on payments for services performed entirely outside Colombia (DIAN Oficio 28380 of 2019)
Configuration requirements.
A residence flag with an effective date on the employee record.
Re-evaluation every payroll period, not annually.
A presence-day accumulator rolling over any 365-day window — the test is not calendar-year based.
A prospective-only switch. The engine must never restate prior months.
Where the year straddles the threshold, residence attaches to the year in which the 183rd day falls.
Citizenship change — far less consequential. Colombian nationality does not by itself determine tax residence for the presence test, and it does not change any contribution rate. What it changes:
Aspect
Effect
Identity document
From a foreigner identity card (code 22) or permit to a citizen identity card (code 13), with a new document number. This is a master-data change that must be reported as a change event on the contribution return and must be reflected in the electronic payroll XML and the annual certificate
Foreign pension-system waiver
A foreign national who had waived Colombian pension affiliation under Decree 1072 of 2015 art. 2.2.1.2.1.4 on proof of foreign coverage should be reviewed on naturalisation
Tax residence
Article 10's nationality-plus-connection tests become available. A Colombian national is resident where the spouse, dependants, 50%+ of assets, 50%+ of income or the place of asset administration is in Colombia — tests that do not apply to a foreign national
Contribution rates
None
The migration-status case, which is more common in practice. A change from a Special Permanence Permit (code 47) or a Temporary Protection Permit to a foreigner identity card (code 22) is also a document-number change requiring the same reporting. Note the open item recorded in Module 2.2: the electronic-payroll annex has no code for the Temporary Protection Permit, and code 47 is the only available proxy.
Are there specific rules for expatriates or inpatriates?
Colombia has no expatriate tax regime, no inpatriate concession and no impatriate relief. There is no reduced rate, no exempt allowance, no tax-free housing or schooling benefit, and no time-limited concession for inbound assignees. Expatriates are taxed under the ordinary rules, with residence as the only switch.
Aspect
Rule for an inbound assignee
Labour law
Fully applicable. Labour Code art. 2 applies the Code to all work performed in Colombian territory, whatever the employee's nationality. Law 2466 of 2025 art. 37: "Immigration status shall not be an impediment to claiming labour guarantees" — migration status is no bar to labour rights
Withholding tax
Flat 20% under art. 408 until residence is acquired; the art. 383 table thereafter. See Module 6
Health and occupational risk
Compulsory, with no waiver
Pension
Compulsory, unless the employee proves affiliation to a pension system abroad and elects in writing not to affiliate — Decree 1072 of 2015 art. 2.2.1.2.1.4
Totalisation agreements
Colombia has bilateral social-security agreements with Spain, Chile, Uruguay, Argentina, Ecuador and Peru, and is party to the Ibero-American Multilateral Social Security Convention. A posted worker with a certificate of coverage may remain covered at home for the certified period. Colombian occupational-risk cover is nonetheless required for work performed in Colombia
Double-tax treaties
Applied only on written client instruction with a residence certificate
Expatriate allowances — housing, schooling, cost-of-living, hardship, home leave
Fully taxable. No exemption exists. They are salary unless a valid written non-salary pact under Labour Code art. 128 applies, in which case they are out of the contribution base up to the 40% clamp
Tax equalisation and gross-up
Permitted contractually. The grossed-up amount is itself taxable income, so the calculation is iterative. No statutory recognition — a purely commercial arrangement
Split payroll
The Colombian entity withholds only on what it pays or bears. See the foreign-sourced income question above
Work permit and visa
A precondition of lawful employment, not a payroll parameter, but the visa type drives the available identity-document code
Vacation accumulation
Labour Code art. 190 allows 4 years' accumulation for "foreign nationals who render their services in places other than those of their family members' residence" — one of the few express expatriate provisions in Colombian law
Termination
Identical to a Colombian national. All statutory benefits and indemnities apply in full
Transnational teleworkers
Law 2466 of 2025 art. 54: a contract concluded in Colombia is governed by Colombian law, and the occupational-risk insurer must guarantee occupational cover in the country where the service is performed, with additional foreign insurance at the employer's cost where needed
The single largest expatriate configuration risk remains the residence switch. A COP 15,000,000 salary produces COP 3,000,000 of withholding as a non-resident and COP 1,190,532 as a resident — a 2.5-fold difference that turns on one date. See the worked example in Module 6.3.
Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 2, 59A, 62, 67, 68, 69, 70, 71, 72, 128, 129, 159, 162, 173 num. 5, 190 (art. 59A added by Law 2466 of 2025 art. 48); Law 100 of 1993 art. 18 par. 1; Law 1955 of 2019 art. 244; Law 2466 of 2025 arts. 34, 37, 45, 48, 54; Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 9, 10, 55, 56, 114-1, 254, 383 (pars. 2 and 3), 386, 388, 408; Law 2277 of 2022 art. 8; Decree 1072 of 2015 art. 2.2.1.2.1.4; DIAN Concepto 011108 of 29 June 2026; DIAN Oficio 28380 of 2019; Andean Community Decision 578; the Ibero-American Multilateral Social Security Convention.

The ordering effect made visible. Had the 25% exemption been computed on the pre-deduction figure — 25% × 10,920,000 = 2,730,000 — the total deductions would have been 4,730,000, capped at 4,368,000, giving a base of 6,552,000 = 125.1002 tax units and a withholding of COP 299,529.30. The correct ordering produces COP 325,749.30. The difference of COP 26,220.00 per month is the cost of the ordering error, and it compounds across a workforce.

Note on the class of relief. The voluntary pension and long-term savings contributions are exempt income (Tax Code arts. 126-1 and 126-4), deducted inside the 40% and 1,340-unit caps. Had the same COP 2,000,000 been paid instead as a voluntary contribution to the individual pension account under art. 55, it would have been non-taxable income, deducted before the cap reference figure was struck — a structurally more valuable relief. The engine must carry the two vehicles as distinct wage types.
Example 4 — Employee with a student loan and a special statutory deduction
Facts. Salary COP 3,500,000 — just below the 2-minimum-wage transport-allowance ceiling of COP 3,501,810, so the allowance is due. The employee repays a national student loan through payroll under a written payroll-lending authorisation at COP 350,000 per month, and is subject to the Higher Education Solidarity Contribution (Contribución Solidaria a la Educación Superior — Contribución Sabes) notified at COP 120,000 per month.
Scope note. Colombia has no statutory student-loan repayment tax and no income-contingent repayment scheme deducted at source as a tax. The two Colombian analogues are (a) repayment of a national student loan through a payroll-lending authorisation under Law 1527 of 2012 — a voluntary, employee-authorised deduction, and (b) the Higher Education Solidarity Contribution, which Labour Code art. 150, as substituted by Law 1911 of 2018 art. 22, expressly makes a permitted deduction requiring no individual written order. Neither is a tax and neither reduces the withholding base.

Example 5 — Complex scenario: all-inclusive salary above the exemption threshold
Facts. Senior executive on an all-inclusive salary (salario integral) of COP 25,000,000 per month under a written agreement, indefinite-term contract, occupational risk class I. This is the most structurally distinctive Colombian remuneration form, and it engages four separate rules at once.
Step 0 — Validity check

Step 4 — The exemption test
The employee earns COP 25,000,000, which is at or above 10 minimum wages (COP 17,509,050). The Tax Code art. 114-1 exemption is therefore LOST. Note carefully that the test is applied to what the employee earns — the full COP 25,000,000 — not to the 70% contribution base of COP 17,500,000, which would (barely) have fallen below the threshold. Testing on the contribution base would wrongly exonerate this employee and understate employer cost by roughly COP 2.36 million a month.

The four lessons this example carries.
The 70% rule applies to the contribution base and the payroll-levy base, not to tax. The whole
COP 25,000,000 is taxable.
The exemption test is on what the employee earns, not on the contribution base. Testing on the 70%
figure would have wrongly exonerated the employee.
Vacation is not absorbed and is owed on the full COP 25,000,000, while contributions run on 70%.
Three different bases in one employee: 25,000,000 for tax and vacation, 17,500,000 for contributions and levies.
The employer cost factor is lower — 1.2518 against 1.3579 for a minimum-wage employee — because the
70% rule and the absorption of three of the four statutory benefits outweigh the loss of the exemption. This is why the all-inclusive salary is the standard senior-executive structure in Colombia.

Note on example 3: net pay is after the employee's own COP 2,000,000 of voluntary savings contributions, which are a transfer to the employee's own accounts, not a cost. Employer cost is not shown because the example is constructed to isolate the depuration ordering rule.
The Colombian employer cost factor is not monotonic in salary. It rises at the low end because the contribution-base floor bites on part-time and low-paid work; it falls at the top end because of the all-inclusive salary's 70% rule; and it spikes at exactly 10 minimum wages where the exemption is lost. The cliff is quantified in Module 5 and demonstrated by the bonus example in Module 9.2.
Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 127, 128, 129, 132, 149, 150, 151, 192, 230, 249, 306 (art. 150 as substituted by Law 1911 of 2018 art. 22); Law 100 of 1993 arts. 18, 20, 204; Law 21 of 1982 art. 17; Law 89 of 1988 art. 1; Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30; Law 1527 of 2012; Tax Code (Decree-Law 624 of 1989) arts. 55, 56, 114-1, 126-1, 126-4, 206 num. 10, 336 num. 3, 383, 385, 387, 388; Decree 1072 of 2015 arts. 2.2.4.3.3 and 2.2.4.3.5; Decree 1772 of 1994 art. 13; Decree 780 of 2016 art. 3.2.1.5 (substituted by Decree 1990 of 2016 art. 1); Decree 1469 of 2025; Decree 1470 of 2025; Resolution DIAN 000238 of 2025.

Module 13: Common Business Practices & Non-Statutory Rules
Purpose: To capture prevalent, non-statutory conventions.
Status of this module. Everything below is market convention, not statute, except where a statutory constraint is expressly identified. These are the practices a Colombian payroll implementation will encounter and must be able to configure.
13.1 Non-Statutory Conventions
Describe the common practice for off-cycle or ad-hoc payments.
Off-cycle payments are common in Colombia and are constrained by one structural fact: the electronic payroll document and the social-security contribution return are both monthly. An off-cycle payment therefore never creates a separate filing — it is aggregated into the same monthly artefacts.
Practice
Prevalence
Handling
The semi-monthly advance
Dominant. The 15th is paid as a fixed advance of half the basic salary, with no withholding tax and no social-security deduction; the month-end payment settles everything
Not truly off-cycle — it is the standard cycle. Both payments are aggregated into one monthly contribution base, one monthly withholding calculation and one monthly XML
Termination settlement paid off-cycle
Very common — settlements are paid within days of termination rather than waiting for the cycle
Aggregated into the termination month's base for both tax and social security
Off-cycle bonus
Common — annual and performance bonuses are often paid on their own date
Aggregated into the month. Tax effect = table(new month total) − already withheld
Payroll error correction
Common
Where a payment was short, pay the shortfall off-cycle. Do not defer it — Labour Code art. 65 exposes the employer to one day's salary per day of delay at termination
Salary advance (anticipo de salario)
Common, typically 30% to 50% of the month's salary, recovered from the next payment
Requires a written agreement stating the instalment and the amortisation period (Labour Code art. 151). Not a loan — an advance on salary already accruing
Emergency or hardship payment
Occasional
Treated as an advance, or as an occasional gratuity (non-salary under art. 128)
Off-cycle statutory benefit payment
Occasional — the service bonus and severance-savings settlement are frequently paid on their own dates
Statutory dates: 30 June and 20 December for the service bonus; January for the interest; 14 February for the severance-savings deposit
Rules that govern any off-cycle payment.
Aggregate into the month, do not create a second monthly artefact. One contribution return record,
one XML document, one Form 350 line per employee per month. Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 3 and DIAN Concepto 013840 (int. 1568) of 1 October 2025 are both explicit on monthly accumulation.
Compute the incremental tax, not a fresh table read. Under Procedure 1 the correct withholding on
the second payment is table(month total) − already withheld. A fresh read on the off-cycle payment alone applies the progressive bands twice from zero and systematically under-withholds.
Re-test the monthly thresholds on the new total. The 10-minimum-wage exemption cut-off, the
4-minimum-wage solidarity trigger, the 16-minimum-wage subsistence bands, the 25-minimum-wage ceiling and the 40% non-salary clamp are all monthly tests on the month's aggregate.
Transmit the XML once, by the 10th of the following month, covering all payments of the month.
Never use an off-cycle payment to defer a statutory date. The service-bonus and severance-savings
dates are hard deadlines with day-rate penalties.
A practical convention worth recording. Because the 15th advance carries no deductions, an employee who terminates between the 1st and the 15th will have received an advance against a month that will not be completed. The settlement must recover it, and the recovery is subject to the minimum-wage floor of Labour Code art. 149 num. 2 — so a large advance against a short final month can be irrecoverable through payroll and must be pursued separately.
How are non-recoverable draws against future commissions typically handled?
Commission arrangements are common in Colombian sales, insurance, real-estate and pharmaceutical roles. Commissions are salary by express statutory definition — Labour Code art. 127 lists "porcentajes sobre ventas y comisiones" — which shapes everything about how draws are handled.
Practice
Description
Payroll treatment
Guaranteed minimum plus commission
The dominant Colombian structure. A fixed basic salary at or above the minimum wage, plus commission earned on top. There is no draw and no recovery
Basic salary and commission are both salary. Both enter the contribution base, the payroll-levy base and the statutory-benefit base. Commission also enters the vacation base (Labour Code art. 192 excludes only overtime and rest-day work)
Recoverable draw (anticipo de comisiones)
A monthly advance against commissions not yet earned, recovered from later commission
Structured as a salary advance under Labour Code art. 151, requiring a written agreement stating the instalment and the amortisation period. Recovery is subject to the minimum-wage floor of art. 149 num. 2
Non-recoverable draw
A guaranteed monthly amount that is not recovered if commissions fall short
This is simply salary. It is not a loan, it is not recoverable, and it is fully contributory and fully taxable in the month it is paid. Colombia has no concept of a non-recoverable draw sitting outside salary
Commission-only remuneration
Legally permitted — Labour Code art. 132 num. 1 allows remuneration by unit of time, work, piece or task
Constrained by the minimum wage. Where commissions in a month fall below the minimum wage, the employer must top up to the minimum wage. The top-up is salary
Why Colombia rarely uses the classic non-recoverable draw. Four statutory constraints make it unattractive:
The minimum-wage guarantee. The employer must in any event top up to the minimum wage, so a
guaranteed floor already exists by operation of law.
The contribution-base floor. Even a nil-commission month contributes on 1 minimum wage, so
there is no contribution saving in a low month.
Article 149 num. 2. Recovery of a draw may not take pay below the minimum wage without a court
order, even with written authorisation — so recovery is often impossible in exactly the months where it is needed.
Variable-pay averaging. Commissions enter the statutory-benefit bases on averaged figures — the
service bonus on the semester average, severance savings on the average of the last year where the salary varied in the last 3 months (art. 253), vacation on the average of the preceding year (art. 192 num. 2). A draw structure does not reduce those averages.
Handling the recovery of a draw where the employee terminates. The outstanding balance is set off against the termination settlement, but only within the art. 149 limits and only on the strength of the written agreement. Any residue is an ordinary civil debt, pursued outside payroll. Do not withhold the statutory benefits to force recovery — Labour Code art. 65's late-payment penalty of one day's salary per day of delay will exceed the draw within weeks.
Configuration. Carry three distinct wage types: commission earned (salary, contributory, in the vacation base), commission advance (a recoverable payment governed by art. 151, with a balance), and commission guarantee top-up (salary). Maintain the rolling averages the statutory-benefit bases require.
Are there common non-statutory leave types? How are they managed and reported?
Yes — Colombia has an active market in extra-statutory leave, driven partly by collective agreements and partly by employer branding. None of it is regulated, and none of it is reported to any authority.
Non-statutory leave
Prevalence
Typical terms
Payroll treatment
Additional vacation days
Common in professional and multinational employers
5 to 15 additional days on top of the statutory 15 working days
Paid at ordinary salary. If genuinely additional paid rest, it enters the contribution base like statutory vacation. Cash compensation of the additional days is not restricted by the art. 189 half-limit, which applies to the statutory entitlement
Birthday day off
Very common
One paid day per year
Paid leave by employer decision; salary continues; contributions continue
Christmas–New Year shutdown
Common in manufacturing
Collective shutdown, often charged against statutory vacation
Where charged against vacation, treated as vacation. Where additional, paid leave
Extended bereavement leave
Common
Beyond the statutory 5 working days, or extending to relatives outside the statutory degrees
Paid leave; salary continues
Extended paternity leave
Increasingly common
Beyond the statutory 2 weeks, employer-funded
Employer-paid, not recoverable from the health insurer. Fully contributory and taxable
Marriage leave
Common
3 to 5 paid days
Paid leave
Moving-house leave
Occasional
1 to 2 paid days
Paid leave
Study or examination leave
Common in employers with education programmes
Paid or unpaid
As agreed
Volunteering day
Growing
1 to 2 paid days per year
Paid leave
Sabbatical
Rare, senior staff
1 to 6 months, usually unpaid
Unpaid leave — suspension mechanics apply: unpaid-leave change event, levies to 0%, accruals suspended (Module 8)
"Flexible Friday" / short summer hours
Common
Early finish, no pay reduction
A working-time arrangement, not leave. Must stay within the maximum-hours rules
Additional sick days without a certificate
Common
2 to 3 days per year at full pay
Employer-paid; not recoverable from the health insurer, which pays only on a certified incapacity from day 3
Management and reporting.
Aspect
Treatment
Legal basis
Contract, internal work rules or collective agreement. Once granted habitually, an extra-statutory benefit may become a binding condition of employment through the principle of acquired rights, so it should be documented as revocable where the employer wants flexibility
Contribution base
Paid extra-statutory leave is paid rest and enters the contribution base and the payroll-levy base, exactly as statutory vacation does
Statutory-benefit base
Enters the service-bonus, severance-savings and interest bases as ordinary salary
Tax
Ordinary taxable employment income
Statutory reporting
None. No authority receives extra-statutory leave data. It appears in the electronic payroll XML only as ordinary remunerated leave or as part of the ordinary salary line
Vacation register
Labour Code art. 187 num. 3 requires a register of statutory vacation. Extra-statutory days should be tracked separately, so that the statutory 15 working days can be evidenced and the art. 190 accumulation limits applied to the right balance
Termination
Extra-statutory leave is not paid out on termination unless the contract or agreement says so. Only the statutory vacation entitlement must be settled in cash
The configuration rule that matters. Keep two separate balances — statutory vacation and extra-statutory leave. They have different accrual rules, different accumulation limits, different cash compensation limits and, critically, different termination payout obligations. Merging them into one balance creates an unfunded termination liability on the extra-statutory portion and makes the statutory 15-day entitlement impossible to evidence.
Other prevalent non-statutory practices worth recording
Practice
Prevalence
Notes
Non-salary pacts on a substantial share of remuneration
Very widespread. The standard Colombian remuneration-structuring device
Reduces the contribution base and the statutory-benefit base, up to the 40% clamp of Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30. No tax effect whatsoever. Employers commonly structure to just under 40%
All-inclusive salary for senior staff
Standard above 13 minimum wages
Contribution and payroll-levy bases at 70%; three of the four statutory benefits absorbed; vacation is not
Employer-paid prepaid health cover
Very common for professional staff
Structured as non-salary; the employee may deduct up to 16 tax units per month against the withholding base
Meal vouchers and meal cards
Common
Tax Code art. 387-1: not income up to 41 tax units per month where salary ≤ 310 tax units. Must be paid to a third party — cash for food is salary
Employer-funded voluntary pension contributions
Common for senior staff
Exempt income within the shared 30% / 3,800-unit cap; deductible for the employer up to 3,800 units per employee
Extra-statutory Christmas bonus (prima extralegal de navidad)
Common
Set off against the statutory service bonus under Labour Code art. 308; the employer pays only the difference where the statutory bonus is larger
Extra-statutory vacation bonus (prima de vacaciones)
Common
Usually structured as non-salary under a written art. 128 pact
Payroll lending (libranza)
Extremely widespread — a mainstream consumer-credit channel in Colombia
Law 1527 of 2012. The employer must deduct and remit; the aggregate must leave at least 50% of net salary. Employers routinely manage several concurrent lenders per employee
Employee savings funds (fondos de empleados)
Very common in larger employers
Employee contributions deducted under Labour Code art. 150 without an individual order; employer contributions usually structured as non-salary
Monthly advance deposit of severance savings
New and growing
Law 2466 of 2025 art. 64 permits monthly deposit of 8.33% to the fund, and written agreement to pay the 1% interest monthly. Smooths the February and January cash-flow spikes
Advancing health-insurer benefits through payroll
Universal practice
The employer advances sickness, maternity and paternity benefits and recovers from the insurer, because the institution pays in arrears. The engine must model a benefit line that is paid to the employee but receivable from the institution
Paying the 100% rest-day premium early
Occasional
Labour Code art. 179 transitional paragraph expressly permits an employer to adopt the 100% premium ahead of the 2027 date. Where it does, DIAN Concepto 013840 of 2025 confirms the differentiated percentages can coexist within a month without obstructing the electronic payroll filing
Thirteenth and fourteenth salaries
Not a Colombian practice
Colombia's 13th-month equivalent is the statutory service bonus, paid in two halves. There is no 14th salary
Tax equalisation for expatriates
Common in multinationals
Purely contractual; no statutory recognition. The grossed-up amount is itself taxable, so the calculation is iterative
Legal basis: Substantive Labour Code arts. 65, 127, 128, 132, 149, 150, 151, 179, 187 num. 3, 189, 190, 192, 253, 306, 307, 308; Law 1393 of 2010 art. 30; Law 1527 of 2012; Law 2466 of 2025 arts. 14 and 64; Tax Code arts. 126-1, 206 num. 10, 387, 387-1, 388; Resolution DIAN 000013 of 2021 art. 3; DIAN Concepto 013840 int. 1568 of 1 October 2025.
